# Add 66 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Add 66 reviewed retro projects across home computers, consoles, PCs, operating systems, applications and development tools.

Date: 2026-10-18 · Review: ready

Snapshot check: upstream heads were not checked; pinned evidence is a historical snapshot.

## Uncertainty and next checks

- oricopenlibrary-native-support-suite / Runtime profiles (no-evidence-found): README names the Oric family; individual modules have distinct ROM/DOS assumptions. No uniform machine/drive minimum or hardware test was verified.

Suite of Oric BASIC/Sedoric bindings, joystick support, ROM identification, QR generation and font rendering with native tests.

QR implementation includes Reed-Solomon/mask generation and native rendering; BASIC bindings and font routines establish substantial reusable implementation. Treat modules as one library suite, not separate projects. Root GPL-3.0 text. Bundled TTF fonts and adapted QR components have separate provenance that was not exhaustively audited.

Independent interpreter by Johan Berntsson and Fredrik Ramsberg, not an Infocom binary disassembly. Core memory/object/opcode machinery plus per-machine screen/disk backends. Modern independent Z-machine interpreter has no predecessor native binary CPU being reconstructed. The emulated Z-machine is a virtual instruction set; target CPU records native output architectures.

Ruby make.rb drives ACME, Exomizer and VICE/c1541 with configurable tool paths; Python needed for some Z6 graphics assets. Test stories and automated build tests exist but were not run. Native dual-CPU CP/M-compatible CCP/BDOS/BIOS environment translating Z80 program file operations into Apple II ProDOS services.

Substantial 6502 loader/terminal/ProDOS backend and Z80 BDOS/BIOS/CCP code with bank gateway and FCB/DMA transfers. Real SoftCard Z80 executes applications; this is not a CPU emulator. Newly updated default branch inspected at pinned commit. BSD-2-Clause for project code. Bundled assemblers have separate notices; CP/M programs including WordStar/Turbo Pascal/dBase are third-party content and not automatically relicensed by root BSD grant.

### Identity (reviewed)

Native Common Lisp compiler, bytecode VM and runtime for AmigaOS3 and MorphOS, with m68k template JIT, host variants, FASL/heap-image support and an optional Clamacs IDE submodule.
- https://github.com/mdbergmann/cl-amiga/blob/4124e3fd8e8a897724ebb35bd7a7dcf5f6d805eb/README.md
- https://github.com/mdbergmann/cl-amiga/blob/4124e3fd8e8a897724ebb35bd7a7dcf5f6d805eb/README-FIRST.md

### Classification (reviewed)

Inspected core VM dispatch/interrupt handling and actual m68k opcode template emitters. A large implementation/test corpus is present, not merely bindings to another Lisp. JIT falls back to interpretation for unsupported shapes; no claim of complete ANSI conformance. Apache-2.0 LICENSE for implementation. Toolchain and optional editor/dependencies are separate projects with their own licensing.
- https://github.com/mdbergmann/cl-amiga/blob/4124e3fd8e8a897724ebb35bd7a7dcf5f6d805eb/README.md

### Identity (reviewed)

Native Sonic-themed Flappy Bird-style game with per-player movement/lives, collision checks, pipes, power-ups, ranking and multiple game states in substantial main.c. This is unrelated in gameplay and implementation to the older Sega arcade game Flicky.
- https://github.com/slinga-homebrew/Flickys-Flock/blob/61ebe20080a6559e4d2947303c214a2166cf4099/LICENSE
- https://github.com/slinga-homebrew/Flickys-Flock/blob/61ebe20080a6559e4d2947303c214a2166cf4099/README.md

### Classification (reviewed)

main.c implements the game, including lower-work-RAM heap extension and CD/PCM assets. README says soundtrack is not supplied. Twelve-player mode requires two six-player adaptors and a homebrew boot method. Root MIT licence; source also retains Jo Engine notice. Sonic/Flicky-themed art and any user-supplied music require separate rights consideration.
- https://github.com/slinga-homebrew/Flickys-Flock/blob/61ebe20080a6559e4d2947303c214a2166cf4099/LICENSE

### Identity (reviewed)

Eric Auer resident DOS interrupt monitor that blocks selected executable/boot-sector/disk writes and detects attempts to disable legacy antivirus monitors.
- https://github.com/FDOS/fdshield/blob/8fdb089b1f8633c83b5d0774bd21507ab52108db/fdshield.lsm
- https://github.com/FDOS/fdshield/blob/8fdb089b1f8633c83b5d0774bd21507ab52108db/fdshield.asm

### Classification (reviewed)

Actual DOS/BIOS interrupt interception and behavior rules, not a signature scanner. Cannot unload; can halt the DOS session and lose unwritten cached data. Documentation says malware aware of it can disable it in unprotected RAM. Historical software only, no modern security-effectiveness claim. Source explicitly GPLv2-or-later; lsm says GPL. No complete licence file present in inspected root.

### Identity (reviewed)

Eduardo Casino resident National Language Support service loading country/codepage packages and implementing FreeDOS NLS and IOCTL interfaces.

Substantive country-table layout, loader and interrupt services. Compile-time guard accepts FreeDOS NLS 0xFD01/0xFD02 only; TODOs include package-loaded checks, memory handling and disk-read validation. Not a universal DOS NLS compatibility claim. ### Identity (reviewed)

Michael Mrosowski 1995 DOS-to-OS/2 driving-game port demonstrating DIVE graphics, software 3D transforms, multithreading, palette management and a real-time voice mixer.

### Classification (reviewed)

Concrete polygon/car transform code, driving loop and four-voice audio mixing. Source calls it a rough example: minimal error handling, unfinished fullscreen DIVE and direct-port joystick access. Sound was only roughly tested and some Gravis drivers fail; no full game-quality claim. Repository adds BSD-3-Clause with OS2World 2019 copyright, while original source permits free use only insofar as not copyrighted by other parties. dive.pas retains IBM all-rights-reserved header and import libraries are bundled; do not assume blanket BSD covers all contents.

### Identity (reviewed)

Actual historical Research UNIX v7 kernel and userland port to Raspberry Pi, retaining filesystem/process/syscall code with ARM startup, MMU, interrupt and SD-card drivers rather than simulating a PDP-11.

### Classification (reviewed)

Source-derived native OS port, not original untouched V7 archive or general emulator. Later Pi models through Pi3 expected to degrade to one CPU; this is upstream expectation, not verified compatibility. Original port work MIT; historical V7 governed by Caldera grant, v10 malloc by Alcatel-Lucent non-commercial permission per README. Also MIT printf, Apache LLVM compiler-rt and other imported components. Do not describe the whole tree as MIT or unrestricted commercial reuse.

### Identity (reviewed)

Preservation mirror of historical Mark Williams Coherent kernel, commands, compiler/toolchain and documentation repositories, including 386 kernel source and archival RCS/build files.

### Classification (reviewed)

Original-source preservation, not a modern clean-room emulator. Historical source headers retain trade-secret warnings; later holder release is separately documented by the archival publisher. Mixed versions, tools and build context require care. Mirror root BSD-3-Clause has generic copyright. Primary archive publisher explicitly posts Mark Williams code on Robert Swartz behalf under three-clause BSD, copyright 1977-1995; this later grant contextualizes old restrictive source headers. Third-party contents may differ.

Protocol-level Tektronix 4010/4014 and ARDS terminal emulator reproducing storage-tube vector drawing, fading and timing for historical Unix plotting and interactive systems.

Terminal/protocol recreation, not a CPU/full-system emulator or UNIX source port. Historical plot files and PiDP-11 integration are intended use cases, not verified system compatibility claims. Root carries GPLv3 while tek4010.c header states GPLv2-or-later. Preserve this distinction rather than asserting uniform GPLv3-only coverage; historical plot/manual content may have other terms.

## Research event notes

Broad discovery followed platform catalogues, developer portfolios, source-preservation and tooling links, with programmatic URL screening before detailed inspection. This is a diverse bounded pass, not an exhaustive inventory of every platform. Quality and completeness caveats remain explicit; preserved source, native reconstruction, homebrew and modern tooling are distinguished. No candidate code was executed. All earlier catalogue records are preserved.
